# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2021-05-10_TotalExtraction_95706584.pdf
   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
May 10, 2021 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel (via 
Zoom), Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff 
White.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Johnson and Mr. White, agenda item #32 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  
Mr. Roybal moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on April 26, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for land located north of Pershing 
Boulevard and west of Christensen Road.  Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development Department, 
explained they received a petition to annex the land, provided information on the property and 
reviewed requirements of compliance with annexation conditions contained in state statutes.  Mr. 
Lloyd explained agenda items #21 & #22 are affiliated with the annexation and single-family 
homes and duplexes are proposed for the area.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Lloyd explained the intersection 
of Pershing & Christensen is currently outside of city limits.  There being no further comments or 
input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the hearing. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for land located east of and adjacent to 
South Greeley Highway [US 85], north of Chalk Bluff Road. Seth Lloyd, Planning and 
Development Department, explained they received a petition to annex the land, provided 
information on the property and reviewed requirements of compliance with annexation conditions 
contained in state statutes.  Mr. Lloyd explained agenda items #23 & #24 are affiliated with the 
annexation and advised the land is proposed to be used for industrial purposes.  Upon inquiry, Mr. 
Lloyd explained there are approximately 432 acres that will be annexed between the prior public 
hearing annexation and this one.  Concern was expressed with expanding emergency services to 
annexed areas.  Mr. Lloyd advised Police Department and Fire Department are asked to review 
annexations and plats and he doesn’t believe there were any comments made, noting however, the 

   
absence of comments doesn’t mean there won’t be any concerns with expanding services.  
Comment was stressed that the city needs to make sure services can be covered when new areas 
are annexed.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been 
met, Mayor Collins closed the hearing. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for land located southwest of the 
intersection of Braehill Road, Parkhill Road, and Panorama Drive. Seth Lloyd, Planning and 
Development Department, explained they received a petition to annex the land, provided 
information on the property and reviewed requirements of compliance with annexation conditions 
contained in state statutes.  Mr. Lloyd explained agenda items #25 & #26 are affiliated with the 
annexation and duplex development is proposed for the area. There being no further comments or 
input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the hearing. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a bar and grill liquor license application filed 
with the City Clerk’s Office for HRARG DH Cheyenne, WY, LLC, d/b/a Dog Haus, 3838 Atkin 
St., Cheyenne Wyoming.  Kris Jones, City Clerk, upon inquiry, confirmed the current owners hold 
a bar and grill liquor license and advised bar and grill liquor licenses are not transferable and this 
request is due to change in ownership.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of 
the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the hearing and referred the application to 
Finance Committee. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a restaurant liquor license application filed with 
the City Clerk’s Office for Tres Amigos Incorporated d/b/a Tres Amigos Family Mexican 
Restaurant, 2414 W. Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming.  There being no comments or input and 
purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the hearing and referred the 
application to Finance Committee. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located northwest of and adjacent to the intersection of Dell Range Boulevard and Whitney Road.  
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
(#4358) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County A-2 Agricultural to AG Agricultural for 
land located northwest of and adjacent to the intersection of Dell Range Boulevard and Whitney 
Road.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4359) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic 
Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Municipal Code 
of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to 
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#4360) 

   
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Rescinding Footnote 1 in Ordinance No. 4314 
appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming for the various purposes in conducting 
the municipal government of said city and fixing the amount of General and Special Taxes as part 
of the revenue required to meet the said appropriation, all for the fiscal year beginning July 1, 
2020, and ending June 30, 2021.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
(#4361) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the City of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste Fee 
Schedule.  Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public Works, explained this is the annual amendment of 
the fee schedule and provided information on the areas of increase.  Ms. Nemecek reported that 
residential trash and recycling services are subsidizing other services.  She provided details on 
proposed changes which include: removal of 1.5 yard waste containers, removal of acceptance of 
single items at transfer station, increased fee for 1.5 cubic yard container cleaning, increased fee 
for disposition of tires based on weight, and removal of “There is no guarantee the optional yard 
waste collection program will be available upon return” sentence in Note 11.  Ms. Nemecek 
advised that upon request from members of the governing body, she will work on a media 
campaign for informational purposes for the solid waste program.  Ms. Nemecek provided 
clarification on removal of 1.5 cubic yard waste containers and the increase in cleaning fee of the 
1.5 cubic yard container.  Upon further inquiry, Ms. Nemecek advised over the years they have 
been looking at whether containers are picked up on the street or in an alley and explained that 
pick up location is consistent per block and/or neighborhood.  Ms. Nemecek explained, upon 
inquiry, that there was a previous increase for the yard waste program of $1.20 so they could invest 
in more equipment, drivers and bins so the program could be expanded, and bins are available. 
She further explained since the program fee increase, money is not being lost on the program, but 
services aren’t being used efficiently as there aren’t enough subscribers.  Additional drivers and 
equipment are being utilized in other areas of the solid waste program for additional pick-ups, etc. 
During discussion related to Colorado taking plastic numbers 1-6, it was noted the expectation is 
plastics recycling overall will decrease, not increase.  Appreciation was expressed to Ms. Nemecek 
for working on the PSA regarding recycling.  Ms. Nemecek provided information on the “Recycle 
Coach” app which can assist customers.  Upon further inquiry related to why plastic recycling isn’t 
expanding, Ms. Nemecek advised contractors typically are not willing to take a loss due to 
accepting materials they will lose money on such as plastics.  Ms. Nemecek noted tin cans, 
cardboard and glass provide a positive return, however, to accept glass, they would have to increase 
program fees.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White.   Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
increase the total lot coverage of the MD1, MD2, P-CV1, P-CV2, and P-CV3 lot types to 80%.    
Dr. Rinne advised there was no recommendation on the item at the Public Services Committee.  
Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Dr. Rinne reported 
concerns were raised during the Public Services Committee that although the proposal is to allow 
greater building coverage in those lot types, it would leave a negative effect relating to decreased 
landscaping requirements.  Comment was made that caution was raised by the Board of Public 
Utilities relating to water usage concerns involving landscaping water requirements.  Additional 

   
concerns were raised in areas relating to landscaping, primarily trees, which have died due to lack 
of maintenance in areas such as around the airport terminal.  Comments were made that developers 
follow landscaping requirements when properties are developed, but those green areas are not 
maintained after completion.  Concerns were raised related to management and enforcement of 
landscaping requirements and request was made to take this opportunity to look at the current 
landscaping code for possible revisions.   Mayor Collins advised staff is currently working with 
property owners on possible improvements.  It was further advised each development will be 
required to resize runoff facilities to accommodate terrain changes involving landscaping.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with exception of Dr. Aldrich, Mr. 
Laybourn and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.   
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Creating Chapter 8.29, Hood System Cleaning 
Companies, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. Division 
Chief Byron Mathews, Cheyenne Fire and Rescue Department, reported the creation of the 
ordinance is for hood cleaning contractors to ensure that those contractors who clean hood systems 
are properly licensed, insured and trained.  Mr. Mathews advised this ordinance mirrors the fire 
extinguisher business licensing requirement.  Mr. Mathews, upon inquiry, provided information 
on a recent incident where an individual who did not have proper training provided services to a 
local restaurant and the restaurant equipment was left at high risk for catching fire and food 
contamination.  Mr. Matthews explained that with approval of the ordinance, the fire department 
will know who is providing services and can make sure they are qualified. He reported last year 
there were approximately 10 instances where hood systems were not properly maintained or 
cleaned.  It was noted that the ordinance was drafted at the request and in consultation with local 
HVAC and knowledgeable hood cleaning contractors.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Section 5.12.030, Licenses and Permits-
Types, of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, Business Licenses and 
Regulations, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic 
Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic 
Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Repealing Chapter 2.52, Personnel Commission, and 
creating a new Chapter 2.52, Personnel System, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, of the 
Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsors – 
Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Segrave) 
 

   
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Repealing Chapter 2.92, Personnel System, and creating 
a new Chapter 2.92, Administrative Hearing Procedure, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, 
of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsors 
– Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Segrave) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Granting a non-exclusive franchise to Visionary 
Communications, Inc., within the rights-of-way of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to 
Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to align

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
March 22, 2021 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at the Cheyenne Civic Center and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: 
MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Ken 
Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave (via 
Zoom) and Jeff White.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. White, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Cook and Mr. Roybal, agenda items #21 and 26 (b) were removed from the 
Consent Agenda.  Upon request by Mr. Esquibel, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Cook 
and Dr. Aldrich, agenda items #28 (b) and 28 (d) were removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. 
Esquibel moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on March 8, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Special Meeting of the Governing Body on March 10, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a transfer of retail liquor license application 
filed with the City Clerk’s office for Redwood Saloon, LLC d/b/a/ Redwood Lounge & Package 
Liquors, 2105 E. Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming (license currently held by Ace Majestic, Inc. 
d/b/a Redwood Lounge and Package Liquors).  There being no comments or input and purpose of 
the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the hearing and referred the application to 
Finance Committee. 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.2 Planned Development 
Regulating Plan and Section 5.6.2 PUD-Planned Unit Development District, of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development Code (UDC) amending Ordinance Number 3741, which established The 
Village Planned Unit Development (PUD), to allow mixed use business (MUB) District Use on 
Lot 28 of Block 2 of The Village PUD located southwest of the intersection of Powderhouse Road 
and Old Town Lane.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on 
third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Cook. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. (#4348) 
 

   
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending Chapter 5.68, Body Art Establishments and 
Operations, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Sativa Osiris, Diamond Dog Body 
Piercing Studio, commented on the concerns voiced relating to not allowing gun piercing.  Ms. 
Osiris reported the practice will still be allowed however, with the amendment, all businesses 
providing gun piercing will be required to adhere to body art establishment requirements.  Mr. 
Roybal advised the item failed at the Finance Committee due to lack of a second, therefore there 
is no recommendation from the committee.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Dr. Aldrich. Mr. Esquibel moved to amend by substitute to reinsert 
previously stricken language in Section 5.68.020 under “body art” and “body piercing” definitions 
that reinserts language providing an exemption for gun piercing, seconded by Mr. Johnson.   Ms. 
Osiris inquired as to the hesitancy for requiring these types of businesses to adhere to body art 
establishment requirements, noting skin is still being pierced and she doesn’t support the 
exemption. Ms. Osiris further stated she believes big box stores providing gun piercing services 
should adhere to sanitary regulations as the small local businesses do and reiterated these 
establishments work on carpet and do not have hand washing sinks.  Comments were made in 
support of health and safety concerns and opposition to allowing preference to big box 
establishments.  Comments were voiced that there have been no health concerns reported by the 
Health Department regarding gun piercing by local establishments or big box stores and it was 
noted big box stores hire local employees.  Additional comments were made regarding monitoring 
health concerns involving gun piercing at the State Health level.  Motion to amended carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Laybourn 
and Mr. White voting “no”.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. (#4349) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Section 5.12.150, Suspension or Revocation 
of License; Denial of Permit, of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, 
Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4350) 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Section 5.12.110, Open Container-Sales and 
Consumption-Exceptions, of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, 
Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne.  Alessandra 
McCoy Fakelman, Deputy City Attorney, reported on the ordinance changes for open container 
laws which reflect some of the changes that were allowed last summer.  Ms. McCoy Fakelman 
advised there are a few changes related to rodeo events that change the way that open containers 
align with statute.  She provided further information to changes to wine and champagne involving 
open container allowances and other resolution changes made last summer.  It was noted open 
containers within the downtown district boundaries would be allowed between 8 a.m. and 10:00 
p.m., no glass will be allowed and only malt beverage products are allowed.  Ms. McCoy Fakelman 
further reported the Governing Body retains control by allowing for limitations that can be put in 
place by resolution if necessary.  She reported that certain holidays will always be allowed:  July 
4, Cheyenne Day, and Founders Day or Statehood Day.  Amber Ash, DDA Executive Director, 
spoke in favor of the ordinance and noted she received positive feedback related to open container 
events last summer that were beneficial to businesses downtown.  She advised she would follow 

   
up after events with the Chief of Police, noting there weren’t many concerns with the exception of 
1 minor situation involving a specific individual.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from CB (Community Business) to MUB (Mixed-
Use Business Emphasis) for property generally located at the southwest corner of the intersection 
of McCann Ave. and E. Pershing Blvd.  Christofer Lovas, AVI, agent for owner and applicant, 
advised the zone change is for Health Works which is a non-profit medical facility that provides 
care for under insured.  Mr. Lovas reported Health Works purchased 4 parcels and the zone change 
will allow them to move their facility closer to Pershing Blvd.  Mr. Lovas requested a 20’ setback 
instead of the 25’ setback that is currently required, noting there are some utility easements existing 
that will require this. Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) 
Section 6.6.3.b.1, Encroachments.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by 
Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located north of Dell Range Boulevard, west of Whitney Road.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on 
second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Comments were voiced related to the large size of the 
annexation and it was noted there are concerns that need to be discussed relating to prevention of 
future drainage issues.   Discussion included the following topics:  prevention of drainage issues 
relating to the platting process, necessity of approving annexation and zoning requests when they 
have met criteria, noting the Governing Body has no legal justification to deny the annexation.  
Concerns were raised involving annexing large parcels of land and how the city becomes 
responsible for providing public safety and lack of planning to increase first responders, police, 
and fire personnel accordingly.  Additional comments were made relating to denial of annexation 
requests that meet the annexation criteria and need for additional housing to accommodate 
community growth.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the 
exception of Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County A-2 Agricultural to MR Medium-
Density Residential for land located north of Dell Range Boulevard, west of Whitney Road.  Dr. 
Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  It was noted that this item 
is related to agenda item #14 and if the annexation is approved, the land must be zoned for the city.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. 
Aldrich and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located north of Dell Range Boulevard and east of Griffith Avenue.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve 
on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Dr. Aldrich voting “no”.   

   
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County MR (Medium-Density Residential) to 
MR (Medium-Density Residential) for property generally located north of Dell Range Boulevard 
and east of Griffith Avenue.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. 
Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception 
of Dr. Aldrich voting “no”. 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Cheyenne Police Department to apply for Wyoming 
Department of Transportation, Highway Safety Program, Grant Funding for the City’s Mobile 
D.U.I. Command Vehicle.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6130) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Establishing an Affordable Housing Task Force to assess the current and 
projected future landscape of housing affordability in the City of Cheyenne.  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried).  (#6131) 
 
 
RESOLUTION – Supporting and recommending the United States Congress propose and 
send to the states for ratification, an amendment to the United States Constitution clarifying the 
distinction between the rights of natural persons and the rights of corporations, unions, and other 
legal entities, and providing Congress and the State Legislatures the right to regulate monies raised 
and spent for political purposes.  Brenden McKinney, Wyoming Promise board member, advised 
the purpose of the resolution is to address and ratify through our constitution that people have the 
same rights as corporations when it comes to elections.  He reported money in politics isn’t 
primarily traceable and dark money has affected elections in Wyoming without the ability to track 
it.  He further noted this is a bipartisan issue and effort and commented on issues related to 
elections in Georgia.  He stated the more this continues the less voice any of us will have in our 
elections and spoke in support of the resolution.  John Rivera, Wyoming Promise board member, 
stated he has been working on this issue for a number of years and had approached some council 
members last year, and expressed appreciation to Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Cook and Mr. Laybourn for 
their assistance.  Mr. Rivera provided detailed information on Wyoming Promise, a bipartisan 
organization, and a petition drive for the cause collecting over 25,000 signatures.  As many of the 
signatures were removed for various reasons, the petition was not submitted to the Secretary of 
State as it wasn’t eligible to be placed on the ballot due to Wyoming requirements.  He noted 22 
states have similar petitions and 34 states are required to get congress involved to propose an 
amendment to the constitution.  Mayor Collins requested speakers to stay on topic while speaking 
to the resolution.  Mr. Rivera stated the purpose of the resolution is to support a change to the 
constitution, noting Congress can’t on its own propose resolutions.  Albert Simpson spoke in 
support of the resolution.  Boyd Wiggam spoke in opposition of the resolution voicing concern 
with the idea of the city lending support at an attempt to erode the first amendment by asking 
Congress to take up an amendment to curtail political speech, noting it is critical that people who 
have issues be able to have financial support to speak out.  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded 
by Mr. Laybourn.  Comment was made that it is unusual for the Governing Body to bring the 
matter up for consideration since there was a determination by the supreme court that corporations 
are people and can donate to campaigns and that it can be deterioration of political process when 
races can be flooded by untraceable money.  Discussion included:  campaign regulations, concerns 

   
with untraceable funding, purpose of those funds, challenges related to large amounts of money 
involved in Wyoming elections, purpose of resolution, 4th branch of government and separation of 
powers, visit from a Florida representative speaking in opposition of a Wyoming representative 
vote, timing of resolution involving the legislature, issue to make a difference at a local level as 
830 resolutions have been passed at local levels.  Comment was made that this type of legislation 
is inappropriate as the purpose of the Governing Body is to handle local issues.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with exception of Mr. Johnson and Dr. Rinne 
voting “no”.  (#6132) 
 
 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the issuance and sale by the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming of 
Revenue Refunding Bonds, Series 2021, in a principal amount not to exceed $5,445,000, for the 
purpose of refunding the City’s outstanding Refunding Revenue Bonds, Series 2012; prescribing 
the form of said Series 2021 Bonds; providing for the payment of the principal and interest thereon; 
ratifying, approving and confirming previous actions of officers and members of the Governing 
Body of the City and employees thereof; and determining other matters related to the bonds.  
Mayor Collins declared a conflict of interest involving a family member working at a bank related 
to the resolution and recused himself from discussion and voting.  Mayor Collins relinquished 
duties of chair to Council President White.  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  
Mr. Roybal moved to amend to by adding Exhibit A which includes the final principal amount, 
interest rates and listing of the bond purchasers, seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  Robin Lockman, City 
Treasurer, provided detailed information on the amendment and explained it is needed because 
bids weren’t received until March 8th.  Appreciation was voiced to Ms. Lockman and the Treasury 
staff for their efforts on the resolution.  Motion to amend carried. Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body with the exception of Mayor Collins who did not vote.  Main motion as 
amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor 
Collins who did not vote.  Following announcement of the vote, Mayor Collins resumed duties of 
the chair. (#6133)

Wyoming Limited Liability Corporation, Richfield, MN, to outline the responsibilities of the City 
and the Developer for public infrastructure improvements related to Whitney Ranch 3rd Filing 
subdivision – referred to Finance Committee.

Association and the Sweetgrass Commercial Owners Association, Cheyenne, to outline 
responsibilities for both construction and maintenance of the Greater Cheyenne Greenway as well 

   
as enhanced landscaping constructed within tracts of land owned by the City and for areas within 
the public right-of-way within Sweetgrass, 1st and 2nd Filings – referred to Finance Committee.

[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Western Research & 
Development, Ltd., Cheyenne, for design and construction management services of the Nationway 
Reconstruction Project (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 
Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7277 between the City of Cheyenne and 4 Quarters 
Excavation and Development, Cheyenne, for the East Cheyenne Community Park, Parking Lot 
and Trailhead Project (Specific Purpose Sales Tax).  Mr. Roybal moved to approve in an amount 
not to exceed $8,100.00, seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  Upon inquiry, clarification was provided on 
the modification amount.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
(#7277) 
  
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-7-21 for a Contract between the City of Cheyenne and Synergy 
Construction, LLC, for the Happy Jack Landfill Front Entrance and Scale House Project (Solid 
Waste Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid in an amount not to exceed 
$2,537,363.00). (#7309) 
 
 
Consideration of Bid #S-10-21 for a Contract between the City of Cheyenne and JTL 
Group Inc., d/b/a Knife River, Cheyenne, for the 2021 City Overlay Part 1 Project (Specific 
Purpose Sales Tax).  Mr. Roybal moved to accept the bid in an amount not to exceed 
$2,116,969.90, seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  Comment was made that multi-million dollar projects 
should be discussed by the entire Governing Body.  Upon inquiry, Wes Bay, Deputy City Engineer, 
explained that although a project may be shorter in distance, it may require additional materials 
and labor to complete. Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  (#7310)

Builders, LLC, for the Dey Avenue Greenway Connector Project (Specific Purpose Sales Tax).

Contractors, Cheyenne, for the 2021 City Overlay Part 2 Project (Specific Purpose Sales Tax).  
Mr. Roybal moved to accept the bid in an amount not to exceed $1,317,698.76, seconded by Mr. 
Esquibel.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  (#7312)

Parks Division (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid 
from Colorado Golf & Turf, Littleton, CO, in an amount not to exceed $39,736.00).

Wyoming, LLC, Casper, WY, for the Capitol Basin – 26th Street Interceptor Storm Sewer 
Extension Project (2015-2018 and 2019-2022 1 % Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 

   
 
[CA] 
Restaurant liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s office for Machapuchare 
Inc. d/b/a Durbar Nepalese & Indian Bistro, 3515 E. Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming.   Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 
No comments were made on the Revenue Report as presented. 
 
[CA] 
City of Cheyenne Naming and Memorial Policy – referred to Public Services Committee. 
(Sponsors – Mr. Cook and Mr. Esquibel) 
 
 
Announcement was made of Public Hearing to be held April 12, 2021 at 6:00 p.m. in City 
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, and via electronic conference 
meeting, to determine whether compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyoming Statute 
§15-1-402 exist for land located northwest of the intersection of Dell Range Boulevard and 
Whitney Road.  Mayor expressed appreciation to Civic Center staff, Michael Skinner, PIO Officer, 
and Jennifer McClelland, Executive Assistant to the City Council, for their assistance in utilizing 
the Civic Center for City Council related meetings during the last 3 months.  Mayor Collins noted 
beginning April 12th, City Council meetings will resume in City Council Chambers.   
 
 
Under other business:  
 
 
 
Amber Ash, DDA Executive Director, requested consideration to provide comments 
relating to agenda item #19- Resolution Establishing an Affordable Housing Task Force.  Ms. Ash 
provided information involving a County Commission Impact Study related to the impact of 
development and how to increase revenue.  She further noted there are challenges that come with 
annexations and that each time land is annexed it ultimately expands the need for water, sewer 
police and fire services in those areas.  Ms. Ash reported there are areas currently available within 
the City with potential for infill development and in the downtown area there is more of an upfront 
cost for development. Mayor Collins confirmed her request to attend the task force meetings.   
 
 
Dr. Aldrich expressed appreciation to first responders, Public Works staff, other city staff 
and contractors who provided tireless efforts during the recent historic blizzard.  It was noted that 
although there is always room for improvement, the first responders and city staff did a tremendous 
job.    
 
 
Upon inquiry, Kris Jones, City Clerk, confirmed agenda item #26 (b), which was removed 
from the consent agenda, was discussed and approved as amended by substitute.   
 
 
Mayor Collins provided details relating to the recent historic blizzard and expressed 
appreciation to city staff and the public and thanked them for their patience.  He extended the 
appreciation to contractors, first responders, and the County and WYDOT for their partnership in 
addressing the issues.  Mayor Collins acknowledged that no matter how many assets you may 
have, sometimes there are not enough locally when major weather events occur.   

   
 
  
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:21 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
March 31, 2021

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2021-04-12_TotalExtraction_89764042.pdf
ORDINANCE NO. ____________________ 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION FROM CB 
(COMMUNITY BUSINESS) TO MUB (MIXED-USE BUSINESS EMPHASIS) FOR 
PROPERTY GENERALLY LOCATED AT THE SOUTHWEST CORNER OF 
THE INTERSECTION OF MCCANN AVE. AND E. PERSHING BLVD.” 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a zoning map 
amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, 
of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”). 
 
Section 2. That on March 1, 2021, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this ordinance, and recommended 
approval of said zone change. 
 
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change that is the 
subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC. 
 
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the aforementioned application 
is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property described as Lots 9, 10, 11 & 12, 
Block 1, Holmes Corner, Cheyenne, Wyoming is hereby changed to MUB Mixed-Use Business Emphasis. 
 
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the Official 
Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as described in 
Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below. 
 
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are severable. If 
any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared unconstitutional, such 
unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and 
phrases. 
 
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and publication. 
 
FIRST READING: 
 
 
SECOND READING: 
 
 
THIRD AND FINAL READING:  
 
 
 
 
 
 
PATRICK COLLINS, MAYOR

The item for consideration is a Zoning Map Amendment. This is approved by Ordinance.

(2 absent), recommended the Governing Body approve the proposed zoning map amendment.   
 
The Planning Commission Staff report is attached and is unchanged.

2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001

(Fax) 307-637-6366 

UDC-21-00005  
 
 
March 1, 2021 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning  of Lots 9, 10, 11 & 12, Block 1, Holmes Corner, Cheyenne, Wyoming from 
CB to MUB  as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria 
for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from CB Community Business District to MUB 
Mixed-Use Business District for 2.51 acres of vacant land to be developed as a Medical Office/Clinic 
including dental and pharmacy development.     
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure.  Development will require infrastructure improvements and investment.   
 
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Mixed-use Commercial. The optimal zoning 
for the proposed future land use designation is the MUB zone. The intended MUB zoning of this 
property is highly compatible with PlanCheyenne.      
Future Land Use Designation

UDC-21-00005  
 
 
March 1, 2021 
 
The proposed zone change is in accordance with the plans and policies outlined in 
PlanCheyenne and complies with review criteria 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
The proposed MUB zoning is compatible with the surrounding area considering the existing 
zoning and the comprehensive plan’s policies for the area. The proposed MUB zoning allows 
many of the same commercial uses as the CB zoning (although more intense uses may be 
subject to a higher standard of review). 
 
The properties to the east and north of this proposed zone change are currently zoned MUB and 
are partially developed as such, with some vacant properties located north across Pershing Blvd.  
The property to the south is also currently zoned MUB, although it is currently developed with a 
mobile home park. CB Community Business exists to the west and are largely developed with 
Community Business – style development patterns.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criteria 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The current CB Community Business zoning allows a wide variety of commercial uses.  The CB 
zone generally has a lower level of design standards than MUB zoning. The MUB zone allows a 
broader mix of uses, including most types of residential uses, but is a little more limiting on 
intensity of commercial uses.  The proposed MUB zoning also encourages structures to be built 
closer to the street with higher design standards. 
 
The property contained in this zone change were platted with an access easement to the south 
and vehicular access to Pershing would be limited. MUB zoning allows buildings to be built 
closer to the northern property line than CB zoning would allow, therefore permitting a higher 
utilization of the property. 
   
The proposed zone change complies with review criteria 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The MUB zoning is intended to have a highly connected street network with smaller blocks. 
Future site development will be reviewed per the site plan review process.  A commercial Park 
Enhancement fee is due for this property in the amount of $2,750 to be paid prior to application 
of building permits.   
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criteria 4. 
 
 

UDC-21-00005  
 
 
March 1, 2021 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The MUB zone is intended to have a diverse mix of uses in a compact development pattern. 
Uses are intended to be integrated with smaller, walkable blocks. The MUB zone is also 
intended as an alternative to CB zoning for a commercial activity center. The subject property is 
surrounded by development and has limited opportunity to create the highly-connected, 
smaller blocks encouraged by MUB zoning. The subject property must meet the applicable 
design requirements of the Unified Development Code.    
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criteria 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The applicant is seeking the rezoning for all four lots with the expectation of developing a 
medical office/clinic facility. Such future development could be accomplished with the CB zone 
designation, but the applicant desires to design their development with the structures closer to 
the street.  The MUB zone designation will allow that in addition to higher design standards. 
Should the specifically-proposed development not be executed, future development on that 
portion of the parcel would have a similar variety of use options. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criteria 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No reviewing agencies provided concerns or recommendations regarding the proposed zone 
change.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criteria 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criteria 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
The application conforms to PlanCheyenne per the discussion with Review Criterion 1. 
 
 

UDC-21-00005  
 
 
March 1, 2021 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning of existing lots, undeveloped land west of 
McCann Ave., on the south side of E. Pershing Blvd. from CB Community Business to MUB Mixed-Use 
Business Emphasis.  The proposal is to develop all four lots of the property for medical office/clinic uses 
including dental and pharmacy.    
 
The current Future Land Use designation for the property is Community Business, and the optimal zone 
for that designation is the current CB Community Business zone. The proposed designation of the MUB 
zone is the suggested zone for the Mixed-use Commercial designation. The developer proposes to take 
advantage of the MUB zoning district that encourages structures to be built closer to the street with 
higher design standards.   
 
The proposed MUB zoning is compatible with the surrounding area considering the existing zoning and 
the comprehensive plan’s policies for the area. The proposed MUB zoning allows many of the same 
commercial uses as the current CB zoning.  
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d) The zoning map 
amendment application is consistent with the zoning of surrounding properties and responds to current 
market demands for the subject property. The zone change meets the review criteria of UDC 2.2.1.(d); 
therefore, staff recommends approval of the zoning map amendment as submitted by the applicant. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation). 
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications. 
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from CB to MUB.    
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Zoning Map 
2. Land Use Plan Map  
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Map

ORDINANCE NO.____________ 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT CODE 
(UDC) SECTION 6.6.3.b.1, ENCROACHMENTS.” 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the UDC. 
 
 
Section 2.  The intent of this amendment is to recognize changing development needs and 
patterns in the City. 
 
 
Section 3.  That on March 1, 2021 the Planning Commission conducted a public hearing, 
reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text amendment. 
 
Section 4.  That sub-section 6.6.3.b.1 is amended to read as follows: 
 
1. Encroachments. 
(a) Where the front building line is built at the front setback line, small 
projections such as bays, balconies, eaves and other minor projections 
integral to the structure and design may encroach up to 5’ in front of the 
front building line, but in no case beyond the front lot line. 
(b) Porches and stoops associated with a front entrance may encroach up to 10’ 
in front of the front setback line provided any roof or associated structure is 
no greater than a single story. See Figure 6-9. 
(1) Shifted porches or side porches may encroach up to 50% of 
the side setback. 
(c) Uncovered Decks and porches may encroach up to 50% of the required 
principal building side and rear setback, provided that no portion other 
than a handrail shall extend higher than 30 inches above the finished 
grade level.  
(c)(d) Encroachments for accessibility such as handicap ramps attached and 
projecting from the foundation are not restricted, except that in no case will 
these accessibility encroachments extend into adjacent properties, easements 
or rights-of-way. Where accessibility issues and pedestrian access along the 
right-of-way are appropriately balanced and the design is consistent with the 
streetscape design, handicap ramps may be approved by the City Engineer 
according to the subdivision waiver procedures and criteria in Article 2. 
(d)(e) These rules apply whether the encroachment as described in the section 
includes a foundation or not. 
     (f) Alternative designs or proposals may be considered by the Board of 
Adjustment through the Conditional Use process of Section 2.2.4. 
By Alessandra at 8:55 am, Mar 03, 2021

 
 
2 
Section 5.  That the aforementioned Section of the UDC is hereby amended as set out 
within this ordinance and sections currently existing within the UDC not set out within this 
ordinance remain unchanged. 
 
 
Section 6.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable.  If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
Section 7.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and

PLN-20-00009  
 
 
March 1, 2021 
 
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
PLANNING COMMISSION  
MARCH 1, 2021 
STAFF REPORT  
 
REQUEST: 
 
A Text Amendment amending the Unified Development Code Section 6.6.3.b.1, 
Encroachments. 
CASE NUMBER:  
PLN-20-00009 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II, Planning and Development Department 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
Section 6.6.3.b.1, Encroachments, as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the 
request meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted by the Governing Body on January 23, 2012, by 
Ordinance Number 3943 with an effective date of April 30, 2012.  The Unified Development Code is the 
regulatory document carrying out the vision of PlanCheyenne and is reviewed annually pursuant to UDC 
1.1.7 [Annual Review Requirement] to determine if any particular standard or regulation is timely, 
relevant and adequately addresses current market conditions. In addition to the annual review, the 
Planning and Development Director and City Engineer are authorized under UDC 2.4.1.b.3 to bring 
forward text amendments on their own initiative.  
 
The current code does not give a clear direction on the allowed encroachment of decks within the rear 
and side yard. The way that staff currently reviews decks is, a deck must meet principal building setbacks. 
The required rear setback on residential lots is generally 20-feet.  
 
DISCUSSION: 
 
UDC Section 6.6.3.b.1 contains the regulations on the allowed encroachments of small projections such 
as bays, balconies, eaves and porches or stoops associated with the front entrance. This section does not 
currently contain regulations on allowed encroachments of decks or porches not associated with the front 
entrance that would be encroaching into a side or rear yard. Currently, staff reviews decks as being 
required to meet the principal buildings setbacks. All single-family residential lots have a required 20-foot 
rear setback, which means that a deck would also need to meet this setback. If a citizen has a home that 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLN-20-00009  
 
 
March 1, 2021 
 
is at the 20-foot rear setback line and would like to add a deck on the back, it would not be permitted 
under current code.  
 
Staff researched the surrounding community regulations regarding deck encroachments. What was found 
is that all communities are quite different in what they permit and how it is written. A few of the 
communities did allow up to 50% of the principal buildings’ rear setback for uncovered decks. Staff has 
determined that this would be acceptable and would allow people to build an uncovered deck to within 
10-feet of the rear property line. The reason for allowing the encroachment for only uncovered decks is 
because should the whole thing be covered with a roof, citizens then start to enclose the deck with walls 
and then you have an enclosed structure within 10-feet of the property line.  
 
Subsection “f” is added so that if a citizen has an alternative design or proposal, they can apply for a 
conditional use which would be reviewed by the Board of Adjustment. The conditional use would be 
reviewed to see if the proposal matches the character of the neighborhood and allows for public 
comment.    
 
PROPOSED AMENDMENTS: 
 
Amend subsection b: 
Subsection “b” explains how far porches can encroach into the front setback. The additions to this 
section are to call out an existing figure that gives examples of porches and to allow the Shifted/ side 
porches to encroach into the side setback up to 50%. This 50% encroachment would be used mainly by 
corner lots.   
 
“b. 
Porches and stoops associated with a front entrance may encroach up to 10’ in front of 
the front setback line provided any roof or associated structure is no greater than a 
single story. See Figure 6-9. 
1. Shifted porches or side porches may encroach up to 50% of the side 
setback. 
 
 
Create subsection c: 
A new subsection “c” would be created to detail the allowed encroachments of uncovered decks and 
porches into the side and rear yard. With the creation of this new subsection, the existing subsection 
that follow will change to the next alphabetical lettering.  
 
“c. 
Uncovered Decks and porches may encroach up to 50% of the required principal 
building side and rear setback, provided that no portion other than a handrail shall 
extend higher than 30 inches above the finished grade level.”     
 
Create subsection f: 
Subsection “f” would be created to allow citizens to propose alternatives to this section through the 
conditional use, rather than a variance. This would allow staff to determine if the alternative compatible 
with the surrounding area.   
 
“(f) 
Alternative designs or proposals may be considered by the Board of Adjustment through 
the Conditional Use process.”  

PLN-20-00009  
 
 
March 1, 2021 
 
REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Review of a UDC text amendment shall be measured by the following three (3) criteria of Section 2.4.1.d: 
 
1. COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  
“The amendment is in accordance with general goals and policies of the Comprehensive Plan, or is 
necessary to address an emerging issue not anticipated in the Comprehensive Plan and is not 
inconsistent with the plan.” 
 
This proposed text amendment is necessary to address an issue not anticipated in the Comprehensive 
Plan when the Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted. The amendment does not change the 
use of residential properties but gives a clearer understanding on how far a porch or deck can 
encroach into the properties yard. The amendment would allow for further encroachment than what 
is currently allowed. 
 
2. STATE STATUTORY REQUIREMENTS:  
The amendment is consistent with the Wyoming Statutes, and specifically the Municipal Planning 
and Zoning statutes. The following Articles are all listed verbatim from Wyoming State Statute. 
 
 
WYO 15-1-601 (a):  
The governing body of any city or town, by ordinance, may: 
(ii) 
Establish setback building lines. 
 
Assessment:  
This amendment proposes modifying the Unified Development Code for 
the purposes of allowing uncovered decks to encroach into both the side 
and rear yard, thus creating a new allowed setback.   
 
3. HEALTH, SAFETY, AND PUBLIC WELFARE:  
“The amendment promotes the general health, safety and public welfare.” 
 
This amendment would not adversely impact the general health, safety and public welfare of 
Cheyenne. 
 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
Staff concludes that this text amendment meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d. 
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
Section 6.6.3.b.1, Encroachments, as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the 
request meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 


--- END OF FILE ---

STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
UDC-21-00032:  Annexation of Whitney Ranch, 3rd Filing  
REQUEST: 
An annexation of 35.7 acres of land  
LOCATION: 
North of Dell Range Boulevard, west of Whitney Road.  
APPLICANT: 
Whitney Ranch, LLC 
AGENT: 
Brad Emmons, AVI, PC  
OWNER: 
Gysel Whitney, LLC 
PURPOSE: 
To allow urban-level residential development 
PREPARED BY: 
Seth Lloyd, Planner II - Development 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

UDC-21-00032  
 
 
March 8, 2021 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.4.2 Annexations 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting annexing of 35.7 acres of land for future single-unit residential development. The 
County Assessor presently shows the tract of land as part of one larger unplatted parcel. The land is currently 
undeveloped in the County. A zone change and plat are proposed concurrently with this annexation. All three 
applications are anticipated to come to final consideration before the Governing Body at the same meeting.  
 
Land annexed to the City requires a City zone. The petition to annex has been accompanied by a submission 
to change the zoning from County A-2 Agricultural to the City zone of MR Medium-density Residential.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is currently zoned A-2 Agricultural in the County. The property is concurrently proposed to 
be annexed and rezoned to the MR Medium-density Residential. The MR zone is generally described as a 
primarily residential zone with compact neighborhood development patterns. The district generally 
allows a variety of residential uses with some smaller-scale civic or commercial uses.      
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
To accept the petition to annex and approve the annexation Ordinance, the Governing Body must make 
the following findings required in WS 15-1-402; a detailed analysis of which is below: 
 
1) An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the city or town.  [WS 15-1-402 (a) (i)] 
 
The annexation of this property will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the occupants of 
future development once it is built. By being in City limits, the property will receive access to 
City Fire and Police and sanitation services. The property will also be connected to City water 
and sanitary sewer instead of having wells and septic systems.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion.

March 8, 2021 
2) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economical and social part of the annexing city or town. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (ii)] 
 
This property, once developed, will constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part 
of the city. The contiguity to the west and south makes the parcel a natural geographic part of 
the City. The development of this property will benefit those who live within the City and will be 
a part of the social and economic fabric that is Cheyenne.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city or town 
and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of the city or 
town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. [WS 15-1-402 (a) 
(iii)] 
 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be made available. Police and fire protection 
will be extended upon annexation. City water and sewer lines exist in the vicinity and could be 
extended with the development of the property. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or town, or 
the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (iv)] 
 
The area proposed for annexation are about 47.8% contiguous, along the western and southern 
edges.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (v)] 
 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed area.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 .   
6) The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected public 
utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a). [WS 15-1-402 (a) (vi)] 
 
A certified mailing was made to the landowners and public utilities on February 5, 2021. The 
public hearing for this annexation is scheduled for March 8, 2021. Twenty (20) business days 
prior to the announced public hearing is February 5, 2021. The earliest confirmation of receipt 
was dated February 6, 2021. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   

UDC-21-00032  
 
 
March 8, 2021 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
The Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne, as updated by Connect 2045, anticipates this area of the 
community to develop with an urban residential style of development. These areas are expected to 
develop with full access to urban services – such areas are best served by being within City limits. 
Policies in PlanCheyenne support development within the Urban Service Boundary and the utilization of 
existing infrastructure.  
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, no comments were made on the proposed annexation.    
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the annexation.   
 
The proposed annexation is a natural extension of the City, is contiguous to the current City limits, and 
services typically available to urban areas are available to this property.   
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the annexation of the property.

1. Aerial, Current City Limits, and Future Land Use Map 
2. Submitted Application 
3. Applicant-submitted annexation map 
4. Supporting photos
